LEVEL 5 CUMULATIVE PROMPT
==============================================================================
Build the highest-performing automated factory possible within the available interaction budget.

Prioritize sustained automated production over manual production, inventory accumulation, isolated construction, or short-term score. Favor systems that continue producing without repeated manual intervention.



Treat failed actions as evidence. Before retrying a failed placement, crafting, insertion, or extraction action, inspect the inventory and relevant entities and verify that every prerequisite is available. Do not repeat substantially the same failed action more than twice by merely changing coordinates or quantities. If the cause remains unresolved, abandon that attempt temporarily and return to productive work using the functioning factory while gathering, crafting, or identifying the missing prerequisite.

Treat the factory as an integrated system consisting of resource extraction, material processing, power generation, logistics, research, and manufacturing. Consider how changes to one subsystem affect the others.


Before every place_entity action, inspect inventory and confirm that the exact entity to be placed is present with a quantity of at least one. If it is absent, do not attempt placement; craft the entity, gather its required materials, or continue productive work with existing infrastructure. Changing coordinates or substituting another chest, furnace, drill, or similar entity does not constitute a new objective. After two failed placement attempts serving the same objective, stop that objective until inventory or entity state has materially changed. The next action must service or expand a functioning production process without repeating the failed placement.

Use the following general progression when appropriate: establish extraction and smelting, secure reliable fuel, generate electrical power, produce science packs, conduct research, unlock electronics and assembly, and then expand into more advanced production. Depart from this progression only when the observed factory state provides a clear reason to do so. 


At the beginning of every response, state one immediate objective using this format:

Objective: [verb + desired factory outcome]

Describe the intended outcome, not merely an item, entity, coordinate, or action. For example, "maintain continuous iron-plate production" is an objective; "wooden chest," "place at (2, -58)," or "use place_entity" are not objectives.

Keep the same objective wording while pursuing the same factory outcome. Changing coordinates, quantities, code, or entity types does not create a new objective when the intended outcome remains the same. If an objective is abandoned, state why and select a different productive outcome.

Before every place_entity action, execute inspect_inventory() and confirm that the exact finished entity to be placed is present with a quantity of at least one. The inventory check and place_entity call must appear in the same executable code block and refer to the same entity. Raw materials such as wood, stone, iron plates, or gears do not count as the finished entity.

If the exact entity is absent, do not attempt placement. Gather its required materials, craft it, or continue productive work using existing infrastructure.

If placement fails, inspect the relevant entities and surrounding area before making one corrected attempt. No more than two placement attempts may serve the same objective. Changing coordinates or substituting another chest, furnace, drill, or similar entity still counts toward the same two-attempt limit.

After two failed placement attempts, abandon that objective until inventory or entity state has materially changed. The next objective must service or expand a functioning production process without repeating the failed placement.

Establish reliable basic production before pursuing advanced capabilities. Preserve and expand working systems where practical. Avoid consuming resources on disconnected, redundant, or incomplete subsystems that do not contribute to factory development. 

This instruction supersedes the earlier placement-recovery rules. Prioritize sustained production over completing a particular construction objective. First service functioning production by extracting output, supplying fuel or materials, and keeping existing machines operating. Expand only when the exact finished entity is already present in inventory and its placement serves a functioning production chain. Do not spend more than two consecutive turns on an unsuccessful objective. If measurable progress does not occur, leave that objective and perform a productive action elsewhere. Chests are optional and are not the default solution to a blocked machine.


LEVEL 4 COMPLETED REVIEW
==============================================================================
# Adaptive Level 4 Completed Review

## Results

- Mean production score: 1,457, down 42%% from Level 3.
- Mean automated production score: 327, down 43%% from Level 3.
- Production variation decreased by 61%%.
- Automation variation decreased by 60%%.
- The runs produced 286 errors, including 273 placement errors.
- Placement accounted for approximately 95%% of all errors.
- Only 6.6%% of errors were recovered.
- Two runs made no additional production during their final 50 turns.

## What Worked

- All three runs established some production early.
- Existing equipment generated automated output.
- Results became more consistent across the three runs.

## What Did Not Work

- The model became fixated on placing chests and similar entities.
- It repeated placement attempts despite the two-attempt limit.
- It did not reliably verify that the finished entity was in inventory.
- None of the 450 turns used the requested Objective statement.
- Lower variation represented a more consistent failure pattern rather than improved performance.

## Level 5 Decision

The detailed placement rules from Levels 2 through 4 will be superseded by a shorter decision hierarchy. Level 5 will emphasize maintaining productive systems and abandoning unsuccessful objectives. Chest placement will not yet be prohibited. A prohibition will be considered for Level 6 if placement fixation remains present in at least two Level 5 runs.

## Review Status

Completed before the Level 5 adaptive runs.
Source batch: experiment_runs/FLE_adaptive_L04_B20260929T182528

==============================================================================

2. Which failure patterns appeared in at least two runs?
